| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Meckel-Gruber syndrome** | `MKS1, TMEM216, TMEM67, CEP290, RPGRIP1L, CC2D2A` | Genetic | `—` | Ocular Associations: Microphthalmos, anophthalmos, chorioretinal abnormalities, hypoplasia/ aplasia of iris, sclerocornea — Occipital encephalocele, microcephaly, multicystic kidney, polydactyly of fingers and toes, hepatic fibrosis, ambiguous genitalia — Causative Gene: MKS1,TMEM216, TMEM67,CEP290, RPGRIP1L,CC2D2A and others |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Renal coloboma syndrome** | `PAX2` | Genetic | `—` | Ocular Associations: Optic nerve or retinal coloboma, optic nerve dysplasia, myopia, strabismus — Renal hypoplasia with end-stage renal disease, multicystic kidneys, vesicoureteral reflux |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Rubinstein-Taybi syndrome** | `EP300` | Genetic | `—` | Ocular Associations: Congenital or juvenile glaucoma, coloboma, nystagmus, strabismus — Broad thumbs and halluces, short stature, DD/ID, facial anomalies, risk of cancerous and noncancerous tumors — Causative Gene: CREBBP,EP300, microdeletions of chromosome 16p |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Warburg syndrome** | `POMT1, POMT2, LARGE1` | Genetic | `—` | Ocular Associations: Microphthalmos, anophthalmos, coloboma, cataract, optic nerve hypoplasia, aplasia or atrophy — Congenital muscular dystrophy, abnormal cerebellar vermis, lissencephaly and neural migration defects, hydrocephalus, DD/ID — Causative Gene: POMT1,POMT2,CRPPA, FKTN,FKRP,LARGE1 |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Anophthalmiaesophageal–genital (AEG) syndrome** | `SOX2` | Genetic | `—` | Ocular Associations: Anophthalmos, microphthalmos — Tracheo-esophageal fistula and esophageal atresia, genital tract malformations in males, renal anomalies, DD/ID, seizures |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Joubert syndrome** | `AHI1` | Genetic | `—` | Ocular Associations: Nystagmus, oculomotor apraxia, retinitis pigmentosa, uveal coloboma, ptosis, strabismus — Absent or hypoplastic cerebellar vermis with ataxia, abnormal breathing patterns, hypotonia, molar tooth sign, cleft lip/palate, seizures, DD/ID — Causative Gene: AHI1,CEP290and other ciliopathy genes as for Meckel-Gruber syndrom |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **OTX2and pituitary abnormalities** | `OTX2` | Genetic | `—` | Ocular Associations: Anophthalmos, microphthalmos, anterior segment dysgenesis, optic nerve and optic chiasm aplasia — Pituitary hypoplasia, ectopic pituitary gland, growth hormone deficiency and combined pituitary hormone deficiency, DD/ID, Arnold Chiari malformation, agnathia–otocephaly complex |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Matthew-Wood syndrome/Pulmonary hypoplasia- Diaphragmatic hernia- Anophthalmia-Cardiac defect (PDAC) syndrome** | `STRA6` | Genetic | `—` | Ocular Associations: Anophthalmos, severe microphthalmos — Pulmonary hypoplasia or aplasia, diaphragmatic hernia, cardiac defects, craniofacial anomalies, DD/ID |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Waardenburg anophthalmia syndrome** | `SMOC1` | Genetic | `—` | Ocular Associations: Microphthalmos, anophthalmos — Limb abnormalities with absent or fused digits (oligosyndactyly), facial anomalies, DD/ID |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **OCA 2** | `OCA2, MC1R` | AR | `—` | Most common type worldwide. 1:39,000 Americans, 1:36,000 to 1:10,000 in African Americans, 1:3,900 in southern Africa. — Classic OCA 2: The clinical features are similar to OCA type 1B. The visual acuity may be slightly better ranging from 20/60 to 20/100 Brown OCA 2: The patients have much more pigment as compared to the classic OCA 2 an |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **OCA 3** | `TYRP1` | AR | `—` | Rare in Caucasians, 1:8,500 in Africans — Affects dark-skinned individuals and is characterized by reddish-brown (copper) skin, red or reddish yellow (ginger) hair, and light brown or hazel irides. It is often referred to as “rufous” OCA. The visual impairment is often milder than other forms of OCA. — TYRP1 — Inheritance: AR — The tyrosi |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **OCA 4** | `SLC45A2` | AR | `—` | Overall 1:100,000; rare in Caucasians and Africans. — Clinical features are similar to Classic OCA 2 — SLC45A2 — Inheritance: AR — The gene codes for a membrane-associated transport protein which transports products of melanin biosynthesis into the melanosomes. |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Hermansky Pudlak syndrome** | `AP3B1, AP3D1, ADTB3A, BLOC1S3, BLOC1S5, BLOC1S6` | AR | `—` | 1 to 9 in 1,000,000 — OCA, bleeding diathesis, pulmonary fibrosis, granulomatous colitis, and/or immunodeficiency. Posterior embryotoxon can be seen in the eye. — AP3B1, AP3D1, ADTB3A BLOC1S3, BLOC1S5, BLOC1S6, DTNBP1, HPS1, HPS3, HPS4, HPS5, HPS6 — Inheritance: AR — Impaired melanosome formation and trafficking. |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Chediak Higashi syndrome** | `CHS1` | AR | `—` | Rare;<500 cases reported. — OCA, easy bruising, recurrent pyogenic infections, neurological manifestations. Pathognomonic azurophilic granules in white blood cells. — LYST/CHS1 — Inheritance: AR — Dysregulation of lysosomal trafficking. |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Griscelli syndrome (GS) AR,Autosomal re** | `MYO5A, RAB27A` | AR | `—` | Rare;<150 cases reported. cessive. — Hypopigmented, silvery hair and skin, recurrent fever, hepato-splenomegaly and pancytopenia. On light microscopy, large clumps of pigment in the hair shaft differentiates GS from the more uniformly distributed pigmentation in CHS. — Type 1 GS— MYO5A Type 2 GS— RAB27A Type 3 GS— MLPH — Inheritance: AR — |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Axenfeld Rieger anomaly** | `PITX2, FOXC1` | Genetic | `—` | Systemic Findings: Axenfeld-Rieger syndrome—hypodontia or microdontia, redundant periumbilical skin or umbilical hernia, facial anomalies — Common Causative Genes: PITX2,FOXC1 |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Anterior segment dysgenesis, including variable KILD** | `FOXC1, PITX2` | Genetic | `—` | Systemic Findings: White matter abnormalities, Dandy-Walker malformation, corpus callosum agenesis, ventriculomegaly, hydrocephalus, hearing loss — Common Causative Genes: FOXC1,PITX2 |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **phenotypes** | `PITX2` | Genetic | `—` | Systemic Findings: Microcephaly, optic nerve drusen, enlarged cisterna magna, flattened sella turcica, cerebral small vessel disease and stroke — Common Causative Genes: PITX2 |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Posterior embryotoxon** | `JAG1, NOTCH2` | Genetic | `—` | Systemic Findings: Alagille syndrome—bile duct paucity leading to cholestasis, congenital heart defects, butterfly vertebrae, facial anomalies, renal anomalies, DD/ID — Common Causative Genes: JAG1,NOTCH2 |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Microcoria** | `LAMB2` | Genetic | `—` | Systemic Findings: Pierson syndrome—small nonreactive pupils, nephrotic syndrome, movement disorder, hypotonia, DD/ID — Common Causative Genes: LAMB2 |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **CHED** | `SLC4A11` | Genetic | `—` | Systemic Findings: Harboyan syndrome—hearing loss — Common Causative Genes: SLC4A11 — There may be utility with the use of topical nonsteroidal antiinflammatory drugs in the treatment of CHED caused by pathogenic variants in SLC4A11 . |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Congenital-onset glaucoma** | `TREX1, RNASEH2A, RNASEH2B, RNASEH2C, SAMHD1, ADAR` | Genetic | `—` | Systemic Findings: Aicardi–Goutieres syndrome—microcephaly, leukodystrophy, cerebral atrophy, intracranial calcifications, hepatosplenomegaly, thrombocytopenia — Common Causative Genes: TREX1,RNASEH2A,RNASEH2B, RNASEH2C,SAMHD1,ADAR, IFIH1 |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **BFSP1** | `BFSP1` | AD / AR | `—` | ssociated Wi: AD/AR — th Isolated Cataract: Cataract 33, multiple types — Cortical, nuclear, punctate lamellar cataracts |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **BFSP2** | `BFSP2` | AD | `—` | ssociated Wi: AD — th Isolated Cataract: Cataract 12, multiple types — Lamellar, cortical, nuclear embryonic, “scattered lens opacities” |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **CHMP4B** | `CHMP4B` | AD | `—` | ssociated Wi: AD — th Isolated Cataract: Cataract 31, multiple types — Posterior subcapsular cataract, progressing to affect nucleus and anterior subcapsular regions. Posterior polar cataracts |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **CRYBA1** | `CRYBA1` | AD | `—` | ssociated Wi: AD — th Isolated Cataract: Cataract 10, multiple types — Cataract congenital zonular with sutural opacities, nuclear, lamellar cataract |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **CRYBA2** | `CRYBA2` | AD | `—` | ssociated Wi: AD — th Isolated Cataract: Cataract 42 — Multifocal cataract, congenital, juvenile, posterior polar, early cataract |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **CRYBA4** | `CRYBA4` | AD | `—` | ssociated Wi: AD — th Isolated Cataract: Cataract 23 — Lamellar and nuclear cataracts |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **CRYBB1** | `CRYBB1` | AD / AR | `—` | ssociated Wi: AD/AR — th Isolated Cataract: Cataract 17, multiple types — Congenital nuclear cataract, pulverulent cataracts |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **AD/AR** | `CRYBB3` | AD / AR | `—` | Congenital nuclear cataract; Cataract 22 — Congenital, cortical, nuclear cataract — Of these gene families, the crystallin proteins represent >90% of the total soluble protein in the lens; pathogenic variants in CRYAA , CRYAB , CRYBB1 , CRYBB2 , CRYBB3 , CRYGC , and CRYGD are frequent causes of nonsyndromic cataracts (Table 21.5). |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **AD/AR** | `FOXE3` | AD / AR | `—` | Autosomal dominant cataracts; cataract 34, multiple types. — Membranous, posterior subcapsular cataracts |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **AD/AR** | `HSF4` | AD / AR | `—` | Cataracts; cataract 5, multiple types — Lamellar, zonular stellate, anterior polar cataracts |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **AD, AR** | `PITX3` | AR | `—` | Cataract 11, multiple types — Cataract 11, posterior polar, total and cortical cataracts |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **AD/AR** | `WFS1` | AD / AR | `—` | ?Cataract 41 — Congenital nuclear cataract |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Branchio-oculo-facial syndrome** | `TFAP2A` | Genetic | `—` | MAC spectrum; nasolacrimal duct stenosis/atresia — Common Extraocular Findings: Branchial skin defects, facial anomalies, and clefts — Small branchial lesions may heal spontaneously |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Branchio-oto-renal syndrome** | `EYA1` | Genetic | `—` | Anterior segment anomalies — Common Extraocular Findings: Branchial arch, ear, and renal anomalies — Screen for hearing loss and renal disease |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Epstein syndrome** | `MYH9` | Genetic | `—` | Cataracts that may be late onset (mean age 37 years) — Common Extraocular Findings: Hearing loss, nephropathy, elevated liver enzymes — Can bleed from thrombocytopenia, may have platelet macrocytosis |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Neurofibromatosis type 2** | `NF2` | Genetic | `—` | Retinal hamartoma and tufts, thickened optic nerve — Common Extraocular Findings: Meningioma, schwannoma, skin lesions — Tumor surveillance is needed |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Stickler syndrome** | `COL11A1, COL11A2, COL2A1` | Genetic | `—` | Vitreous degeneration, high myopia, retinal detachment — Common Extraocular Findings: Cleft palate/Pierre-Robin anomaly, hearing loss, midface hypoplasia, spondylo-epiphyseal dysplasia — Causative Gene: COL11A1, COL11A2, COL2A1 — Specialist care for monitoring for retinal detachment needed |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Gould syndrome** | `COL4A1` | Genetic | `—` | Anterior segment dysgenesis, retinal defects — Common Extraocular Findings: Intracranial hemorrhage/ cerebral anomalies, renal anomalies — Can present with intracranial hemorrhage at birth |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Cerebrotendinous xanthomatosis** | `CYP27A1` | Genetic | `—` | Optic nerve atrophy, premature retinal senescence — Common Extraocular Findings: Infantile diarrhea, neurodegeneration — Treatable if diagnosed early |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **CTDP1-related congenital cataracts, facial dysmorphism, neuropathy** | `CTDP1` | Genetic | `—` | Microcornea, microphthalmia, small pupils — Common Extraocular Findings: Hypo-/demyelinating and distal peripheral neuropathy — May have parainfectious rhabdomyolysis |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Cataract and I/i blood group** | `GCNT2` | Genetic | `—` | Common Extraocular Findings: Adult i phenotype — I/i phenotyping can aid diagnosis |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Cataract/ cardiomyopathy** | `LEMD2` | Genetic | `—` | Common Extraocular Findings: Arrhythmogenic right ventricular cardiomyopathy — Can be associated with sudden cardiac death |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Galactosemia/related disorders** | `GALK1` | Genetic | `—` | Common Extraocular Findings: Jaundice, liver disease, hypoglycemia — Causative Gene: GALT, GALE, GALK1 — Can be detected by newborn screening; detecting cataracts can be useful diagnostically |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Hypomyelination and congenital cataract** | `FAM126A` | Genetic | `—` | Common Extraocular Findings: Progressive neurological impairment, diffuse myelin deficiency — Cataracts are typically congenital and may precede other symptoms. Neurological impairment can manifest as ataxia, spasticity, DD/ID |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Knobloch syndrome** | `COL18A1, ADAMTS18` | Genetic | `—` | High myopia, retinal detachment, anterior segment dysgenesis — Common Extraocular Findings: Occipital encephalocele, scalp/skull defects, DD/ ID, renal anomalies — Causative Gene: COL18A1, ADAMTS18 — Ocular phenotype may be characteristic |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Marinesco-Sjögren syndrome** | `SIL1` | Genetic | `—` | Strabismus — Common Extraocular Findings: Cerebellar ataxia, myopathy, hypotonia — Cataracts can develop rapidly and are not always congenital |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Rothmund-Thompson syndrome** | `RECQL4, ANAPC1` | Genetic | `—` | Common Extraocular Findings: Poikiloderma, skin atrophy, short stature, DD/ID — Causative Gene: RECQL4, ANAPC1 — Early-onset, juvenile cataracts are more likely to be caused by ANAPC1variants |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Spondylo-ocular syndrome** | `XYLT2` | Genetic | `—` | Glaucoma, retinal detachment — Common Extraocular Findings: Osteoporosis, hearing loss, DD/ID, platyspondyly — Treatment with bisphosphonates is effective for some patients |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Warburg micro syndrome** | `RAB18, RAB3GAP1, RAB3GAP2, TBC1D20` | Genetic | `—` | Microphthalmos, microcornea, optic atrophy, small, atonic pupils — Common Extraocular Findings: Microcephaly, spasticity, DD/ID — Causative Gene: RAB18, RAB3GAP1, RAB3GAP2, TBC1D20 — Clinical and genetic overlap with Martsolf syndrome |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Cockayne syndrome** | `ERCC2, ERCC5, ERCC8` | Genetic | `—` | Microphthalmos, enophthalmos, pigmentary retinopathy, optic atrophy — Common Extraocular Findings: Postnatal growth failure, microcephaly, neurological dysfunction, hearing loss — Causative Gene: ERCC2, ERCC5, ERCC8 — Severity is highly variable; symptoms can be progressive |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Huppke-Brendel syndrome** | `SLC33A1` | Genetic | `—` | Common Extraocular Findings: Hearing loss, DD/ID, low serum copper and ceruloplasmin — Serum copper is low in healthy young children less than 6 months of age. |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Hyperferritinemia-cataract syndrome** | `FTL` | Genetic | `—` | Common Extraocular Findings: Elevated ferritin levels — Iron and transferrin saturation can be normal. Can have onset throughout childhood. |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Oculodentodigital dysplasia** | `GJA1` | Genetic | `—` | Microcornea, posterior microphthalmos, glaucoma — Common Extraocular Findings: Syndactyly, facial anomalies, enamel hypoplasia — Secondary glaucoma can present later in life |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Optic atrophy/ 3-methylglutaconic aciduria (Costeff syndrome)** | `OPA3` | Genetic | `—` | Optic atrophy — Common Extraocular Findings: Extrapyramidal signs, spastic paraplegia, ataxia — Monoallelic variants cause cataracts and optic atrophy and biallelic variants cause Costeff syndrome |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Spastic paraplegia, cutis laxa** | `ALDH18A1` | Genetic | `—` | Common Extraocular Findings: Spastic paraplegia, DD/ID, hypermobile joints, increased skin elasticity — Metabolic abnormalities, hyperammonemia, prolinemia, ornithinemia |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Wolfram syndrome** | `WFS1` | Genetic | `—` | Optic atrophy — Common Extraocular Findings: Diabetes mellitus, diabetes insipidus, hearing loss, DD/ID — Diabetes mellitus typical presents in childhood, cataracts are rare |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Marfan syndrome** | `FBN1` | Genetic | `—` | Ectopia lentis, microspherophakia, high myopia — Aortic dilatation, thin body habitus mitral valve prolapses — FBN1, ADAMTS10, ADAMTS17: FBN1 — FBN1variant testing is positive in>90% patients who fulfill diagnostic criteria |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Alport syndrome** | `COL4A3, COL4A4, COL4A5` | Genetic | `—` | Microspherophakia, lenticonus — Hematuria/renal disease, hearing loss — FBN1, ADAMTS10, ADAMTS17: COL4A3, COL4A4, COL4A5 — Hearing loss frequently precedes renal disease |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Centrosomal protein 290 kDA** | `CEP290` | Genetic | `—` | Function: Photoreceptor ciliary transport — Frequency: 15–20% — Can be associated with renal disease |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Guanylate cyclase-1** | `GUCY2D` | Genetic | `—` | GUCY2D* — Function: Phototransduction — Frequency: 10–20% — Relatively severe clinical findings; can have significant photophobia |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Retinol dehydrogenase 12** | `RDH12` | Genetic | `—` | Function: Retinoid cycle — Frequency: 10% — Early-dense intraretinal pigment migration. Maculopathy and early-onset severe retinal dystrophy are more common than Leber’s congenital amaurosis |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Crumbs homologue 1** | `CRB1` | Genetic | `—` | Function: Photoreceptor morphogenesis — Frequency: 10% — Nummular pigmentation, maculopathy, relative preservation of para-arteriolar retinal pigment epithelium, retinal thickening and loss of lamination |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Retinoid isomerase** | `RPE65` | Genetic | `—` | Function: Retinoid cycle — Frequency: 5–10% — Profound night-blindness from birth |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Inosine 5′-monophosphate dehydrogenase 1** | `IMPDH1` | AD | `—` | Function: Guanine synthesis — Frequency: 5% — Autosomal dominant inheritance, macular involvement in the first decade |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Retinitis pigmentosa GTPase regulatorinteracting protein 1** | `RPGRIP1` | Genetic | `—` | Function: Photoreceptor ciliary transport — Frequency: 5% — Relatively stable clinical course |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Aryl-hydrocarboninteracting-protein-like 1** | `AIPL1` | Genetic | `—` | Function: Phototransduction, protein biosynthesis — Frequency: <5% — Relatively severe clinical findings |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Spermatogenesisassociated protein 7** | `SPATA7` | Genetic | `—` | Function: Photoreceptor ciliary transport — Frequency: 3% — Severe visual loss in infancy, photoreceptor structure in central retina may be preserved |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Libercilin** | `LCA5` | Genetic | `—` | Function: Photoreceptor ciliary transport — Frequency: 1–2% — Severe disease |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **COACH syndrome** | `TMEM67` | Genetic | `—` | Ocular Findings: Chorioretinal coloboma, optic nerve coloboma — Extraocular Findings: Cerebellar vermis hypoplasia, oligophrenia, ataxia, coloboma, hepatic fibrosis — Causative Genes: TMEM67 — Implications: TMEM67variants are associated with numerous clinical presentations, including conditions with retinitis pigmentosa |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Joubert syndrome** | `OFD1, AHI1, CPLANE1, CC2D2A, CEP290, TMEM67` | X-Linked | `—` | Ocular Findings: Retinal dystrophy, colobomas (typically chorioretinal), morning glory disc anomaly, ptosis, strabismus, and/or amblyopia; third cranial nerve palsy — Extraocular Findings: Cerebellar and brainstem malformations (MRI molar tooth sign), encephalocele, hypotonia, DD/ID, episodic abnormal breathing, atypical eye movements, he |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **Senior-Loken syndrome** | `CEP290, IQCB1, NPHP1, NPHP4, SDCCAG8, TRAF3IP1` | Genetic | `—` | Ocular Findings: LCA, EOSRD — Extraocular Findings: Nephronophthisis — Causative Genes: CEP290,IQCB1,NPHP1, NPHP4,SDCCAG8, TRAF3IP1,WDR19 — Implications: A complete renal evaluation should be performed. |
| [Ch 21](chapters/021_Congenital_Blindness.md) | **KAT6A syndrome (Arboleda-Tham syndrome: ARTHS)** | `KAT6A` | Genetic | `—` | Trisomy 21: Tuberous sclerosi |
